### Task 14: The catalog units, the profile, the generated pages

**Files:**
- Create: `catalog/packages/{qmapshack,routino,gdal-bin,mkgmap,mkgmap-splitter,socat,osm-garmin,osm-routino,dem-copernicus,dem-qmapshack}.yaml`
- Modify: `catalog/profiles/navigation.yaml`, `tests/test_verify_tree_launchers.py` (the launcher pin gains `qmapshack`), `README.md` (manifest count 255 → 265; launcher units 26 → 27), `CLAUDE.md` (the layout's package count → 265)
- Regenerate: `docs/packages/` (`gen_package_reference.py`), `docs/profiles/` (`gen_profile_reference.py`), `docs/reference/capability-matrix.md` (`gen_capability_matrix.py`), `docs/reference/parity-coverage.md` (`gen_parity_coverage.py`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the schema (Task 3), the launcher commands (Task 4).
- Produces: the ten units the profile names; `navigation` gains them.

The versions are the candidates measured in Parrot 7 echo on 2026-09-28 (`apt-get download` in the spike): qmapshack 1.17.1-1+b6 (1.21.1 in trixie-backports, which D-038 takes on a machine already installing from there, and the plan names), routino 3.4.3-1, gdal-bin 3.10.3+dfsg-1, mkgmap 0.0.0+svn4923-2, mkgmap-splitter 0.0.0+svn654-2, socat 1.8.0.3. The licence URL for the elevation is the bucket's own readme, which states the Copernicus DEM licence and links it; the Copernicus page it links timed out when checked on 2026-09-28.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the manifests**

`catalog/packages/qmapshack.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: qmapshack
version: "1.17.1"
summary: Offline topographic maps, trails, GPX tracks, elevation profiles and routing on foot
categories: [navigation-maps, gps-gnss]

# Navigation piece 2 (D-061). Measured 2026-09-28 from the archive's own
# .deb: 1.17.1 in Parrot's echo (Qt5), 1.21.1 in trixie-backports (Qt6),
# which D-038 resolves on a machine already installing from backports and
# the plan names. It Depends on libroutino0 (Routino in-process) and
# Recommends routino and gdal-bin, carried here as their own units.
# `socat` and `gpsd-clients` are for the gps-tether launcher.
depends: [routino, gdal-bin, socat, gpsd-clients]

install:
  - install:
      method: apt
      packages: [qmapshack]

# Both run the engine on the PATH (D-059), so the configuration edit and the
# loopback bridge are code the engine owns and tests, never a shell line in
# the catalog.
launchers:
  - name: qmapshack-offline
    exec: hammunition maps qmapshack
    title: QMapShack with your offline maps (qmapshack)
  - name: gps-tether
    exec: hammunition maps gps-tether
    title: GPS position for QMapShack, this machine only (gps-tether)
    terminal: true

update:
  probe:
    method: apt_policy
  strategy: apt_upgrade

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    A map program for planning and recording trips on foot, by bike or on the
    water: it shows the Garmin maps `osm-garmin` builds from your regions,
    draws hillshade and slope from the elevation `dem-qmapshack` prepares,
    lays contour lines over them, plans a route on trails with the Routino
    database `osm-routino` builds, records and edits GPX tracks and
    waypoints, and shows an elevation profile for any track. Everything it
    needs is on the disk, so none of it needs a network.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    A road navigator does not know trails and has no terrain. For a hike, a
    search, a field day on a ridge or an EMCOMM deployment away from roads,
    this is the offline alternative to phone apps and web maps that stop
    working with the phone network. It keeps tracks in files you own.
  prerequisites: >-
    Your map regions set in station config, and the `navigation` profile's
    map units installed, so there are maps, elevation and a routing database
    to show; start it from the `qmapshack-offline` launcher, which adds
    their directories to QMapShack's own configuration first. For your
    position on the map, gpsd with a receiver and the `gps-tether` launcher
    running.
  known_problems: >-
    QMapShack has no offline address search: its search asks online
    services, and Routino routes between points, not addresses. Find the
    address in Navit, then plan the walk here. Routino's foot profile does
    not read `sac_scale`, so an alpine path is routed like a footpath; judge
    the route yourself. It stops at startup with "The specified translations
    XML file did not exist" if /usr/share/routino/translations.xml is
    missing, which apt normally prevents; `hammunition doctor` names it.
    It has no gpsd client, which is what `gps-tether` is for. The launcher
    edits QMapShack's own settings file (~/.config/QLandkarte/QMapShack.conf)
    only to add Hammunition's directories when they are absent, and refuses
    to touch a file it cannot read; QMapShack's own setup dialogs change the
    rest. The launchers run `hammunition`, which must be on your PATH.
    Hillshade drawing has not yet been confirmed on hardware.
  upstream_url: https://github.com/Maproom/qmapshack
  upstream_support: >-
    The project's wiki (https://github.com/Maproom/qmapshack/wiki) and its
    GitHub issues.
```

`catalog/packages/routino.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: routino
version: "3.4.3"
summary: Offline routing over OpenStreetMap data, on foot, by bike or by car
menu_title: Offline route finder (routino-router)
categories: [navigation-maps]

install:
  - install:
      method: apt
      packages: [routino]

update:
  probe:
    method: apt_policy
  strategy: apt_upgrade

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Builds a routing database from OpenStreetMap data (`planetsplitter`)
    and finds routes through it (`routino-router`), using profiles for foot,
    horse, wheelchair, bicycle and motor vehicles. QMapShack links the same
    router in-process; `osm-routino` builds the database it reads.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    Routing that works with no network, over the same map data as the rest of
    the station, and fast: five routes of 10 to 17 km on foot came back in 5
    to 39 ms on one region (2026-09-28).
  prerequisites: >-
    A routing database: `osm-routino` builds one over every region you have
    set. The router takes coordinates, not addresses.
  known_problems: >-
    The foot profile does not read `sac_scale`, so it scores an alpine path
    as it scores a pavement. A route cannot cross from one database into
    another, which is why `osm-routino` builds one over every region. No
    place or address search.
  upstream_url: https://www.routino.org/
  upstream_support: >-
    The documentation at https://www.routino.org/documentation/ and the
    author's contact page on the same site.
```

`catalog/packages/gdal-bin.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: gdal-bin
version: "3.10.3"
summary: GDAL's command-line tools for rasters and elevation data
menu_title: Raster and elevation tools (gdalinfo)
categories: [navigation-maps]

install:
  - install:
      method: apt
      packages: [gdal-bin]

update:
  probe:
    method: apt_policy
  strategy: apt_upgrade

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    The Geospatial Data Abstraction Library's programs: `gdalinfo` to inspect
    a raster, `gdalbuildvrt` to join tiles into one virtual raster,
    `gdal_contour` to trace contour lines from elevation, `gdal_rasterize` to
    draw vector data onto a raster, `gdaldem` for hillshade and slope, and
    many more.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    It is how `dem-qmapshack` turns the elevation tiles into QMapShack's
    elevation and contour map, and the standard way to look inside any
    elevation or map raster yourself.
  prerequisites: >-
    None of its own; the tiles come from `dem-copernicus`.
  known_problems: >-
    `gdal_contour` writes into an existing output file rather than refusing
    it (measured on 3.10.3), so a contour file left from an earlier run is
    appended to; `dem-qmapshack` removes its own before each run.
  upstream_url: https://gdal.org/
  upstream_support: >-
    The documentation at https://gdal.org/ and the gdal-dev mailing list
    (https://lists.osgeo.org/mailman/listinfo/gdal-dev).
```

`catalog/packages/mkgmap.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: mkgmap
version: "0.0.0+svn4923"
summary: Builds Garmin-format maps from OpenStreetMap data
menu_title: Garmin map builder (mkgmap)
categories: [navigation-maps]

depends: [mkgmap-splitter]

install:
  - install:
      method: apt
      packages: [mkgmap]

update:
  probe:
    method: apt_policy
  strategy: apt_upgrade

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Turns OpenStreetMap data into a Garmin `.img` map, drawn and routable by
    the rules of a style. Its `default` style already draws and routes
    paths, footways, tracks and bridleways and reads `sac_scale` and
    `tracktype`.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    It is how `osm-garmin` makes the maps QMapShack shows from your regions,
    with no download beyond the regions themselves.
  prerequisites: >-
    `mkgmap-splitter` to cut a region into tiles first; `osm-garmin` runs
    both.
  known_problems: >-
    It exits 0 while logging SEVERE errors, so `osm-garmin` checks for the
    map it should have written rather than trusting the exit status. The
    address index (`--index`, `--housenumbers`) needs upstream bounds files
    that have no published checksum, so it is not built. Debian's wrapper
    passes no Java options, so it runs on the JVM's default heap, a quarter
    of the machine's memory.
  upstream_url: https://www.mkgmap.org.uk/
  upstream_support: >-
    The mkgmap-dev mailing list (https://www.mkgmap.org.uk/mailman/listinfo/mkgmap-dev).
```

`catalog/packages/mkgmap-splitter.yaml`:

```yaml
name: mkgmap-splitter
version: "0.0.0+svn654"
summary: Cuts OpenStreetMap data into tiles sized for mkgmap
menu_title: OpenStreetMap tile splitter for mkgmap (mkgmap-splitter)
categories: [navigation-maps]

install:
  - install:
      method: apt
      packages: [mkgmap-splitter]

update:
  probe:
    method: apt_policy
  strategy: apt_upgrade

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Splits an OpenStreetMap extract into tiles small enough for mkgmap and
    writes the `template.args` file mkgmap reads to build them into one map.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    mkgmap cannot take a whole region at once; `osm-garmin` runs this first.
  prerequisites: >-
    None; `osm-garmin` runs it on your installed regions.
  known_problems: >-
    Debian's wrapper defaults to a 2000 MB Java heap; `osm-garmin` sets
    4000 MB through JAVA_OPTS, the heap it was measured with. Run from an
    extracted package rather than apt, it fails with NoClassDefFoundError
    unless its Java libraries are on the classpath; apt installs them as
    dependencies.
  upstream_url: https://www.mkgmap.org.uk/doc/splitter.html
  upstream_support: >-
    The mkgmap-dev mailing list (https://www.mkgmap.org.uk/mailman/listinfo/mkgmap-dev).
```

```yaml
summary: A relay between two data channels, sockets, files or programs

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Connects two data channels and copies between them: a TCP listener and a
    program's output, a serial port and a socket, a file and a pipe.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    The `gps-tether` launcher uses it to serve gpsd's NMEA to QMapShack on
    127.0.0.1 port 10110, and it is the general tool for joining a program
    that speaks one channel to another that expects a different one.
  prerequisites: >-
    None of its own.
  known_problems: >-
    A listener with no `bind=` option listens on every interface; the
    `gps-tether` launcher binds 127.0.0.1 so a position is never served to
    the network.
  upstream_url: http://www.dest-unreach.org/socat/
  upstream_support: >-
    The documentation on the project site and the socat mailing list it
    lists.
```

`catalog/packages/osm-garmin.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: osm-garmin
version: "station"
summary: Garmin maps of your OpenStreetMap regions, with trails, for QMapShack
categories: [navigation-maps]

# Derived data (D-061): the engine runs the `mkgmap` converter, an enum it
# implements (mkgmap-splitter, then mkgmap with its default style), over each
# region osm-regions installed. Nothing is downloaded here.
depends: [osm-regions, mkgmap, mkgmap-splitter]

install:
  - install:
      method: derived
      converter: mkgmap
      source: osm-regions
      licence: ODbL-1.0
      licence_url: https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright

update:
  probe:
    method: none
  strategy: reinstall
  cadence_hint: >-
    Follows osm-regions: a region whose downloaded file changed is built
    again on the next install, and one that did not is skipped.

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Builds one Garmin map per region `osm-regions` downloaded, as the
    operator in a staging directory: mkgmap-splitter cuts the region into
    tiles, mkgmap draws them with its default style, which already shows and
    routes paths, footways, tracks and bridleways. The map lands under
    /usr/local/share/hammunition/data/osm-garmin/, where QMapShack's
    launcher points it.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    QMapShack cannot read OpenStreetMap's `.osm.pbf` directly. Building the
    maps here means it happens once per region's data, is recorded like any
    other install, and is undone by `hammunition uninstall`.
  prerequisites: >-
    `osm-regions` with at least one region set in station config, and
    `mkgmap` with `mkgmap-splitter`; all install first. With no regions set
    this unit is deferred by name.
  known_problems: >-
    Measured on one US-state-sized region on 2026-09-28: the split took 48 s,
    the build 126 s, the map is 0.85 times the download, and the staging
    directory peaks near 2.9 times the download while it runs, removed once
    the map is installed. The plan uses those factors and says they were
    measured on one region. There is no hiking cartography: the archive
    carries no hiking style or TYP file, so trails are drawn as mkgmap's
    default draws them. The address index is not built (its bounds files
    have no published checksum), and QMapShack would not read it anyway.
    The map carries the Open Database License obligations of the data.
  upstream_url: https://www.mkgmap.org.uk/
  upstream_support: >-
    The mkgmap-dev mailing list (https://www.mkgmap.org.uk/mailman/listinfo/mkgmap-dev);
    problems in the map data are fixed in OpenStreetMap
    (https://www.openstreetmap.org/fixthemap).
```

`catalog/packages/osm-routino.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: osm-routino
version: "station"
summary: One Routino routing database over all your regions, for routes on foot
categories: [navigation-maps]

# Derived data (D-061): the `routino-planetsplitter` converter parses every
# region osm-regions installed into one database, so a route may cross from
# one region into the next.
depends: [osm-regions, routino]

install:
  - install:
      method: derived
      converter: routino-planetsplitter
      source: osm-regions
      licence: ODbL-1.0
      licence_url: https://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright

update:
  probe:
    method: none
  strategy: reinstall
  cadence_hint: >-
    Rebuilt whenever the set of regions or any region's snapshot changes;
    otherwise left as it is.

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Builds one Routino database, prefix `hammunition`, over every region you
    have set: each region parsed in turn, then processed once, as the
    operator in a staging directory. Installed under
    /usr/local/share/hammunition/data/osm-routino/, where QMapShack's
    launcher points its Routino router.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    Routing on trails with no network. One database over all regions is what
    lets a route cross a region boundary, which Routino cannot do across two
    databases.
  prerequisites: >-
    `osm-regions` with at least one region set, and `routino`. With no
    regions set this unit is deferred by name.
  known_problems: >-
    One region that fails to parse fails the whole database, and the step
    names the region and why; the database already installed is left as it
    was. Measured on one region on 2026-09-28: parsing 22 s, processing
    83 s, the database 0.67 times the download, and the staging directory
    peaking near 5 times it. Routino's foot profile does not read
    `sac_scale`. Whether a route across two regions' boundary works has not
    yet been measured on hardware.
  upstream_url: https://www.routino.org/
  upstream_support: >-
    The documentation at https://www.routino.org/documentation/.
```

`catalog/packages/dem-copernicus.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: dem-copernicus
version: "station"
summary: Copernicus GLO-30 elevation tiles for your map regions, verified
categories: [navigation-maps]

# Data (D-061): which tiles follows the station's regions -- the squares each
# region's Geofabrik outline touches -- resolved at plan time. Each tile is
# verified by a sha256 in catalog/data/copernicus-glo30-pins.yaml, or by its
# object MD5, and the plan says which. `osm-regions` is a dependency so the
# plan always carries the regions the tiles follow.
depends: [osm-regions]

install:
  - install:
      method: dem-tiles
      provider: copernicus-glo30
      licence: Copernicus DEM licence (free, with attribution)
      licence_url: https://copernicus-dem-30m.s3.amazonaws.com/readme.html

update:
  probe:
    method: none
  strategy: reinstall
  cadence_hint: >-
    A tile changes only when the publisher's tile list changes. New regions
    bring their tiles on the next install; a tile no region needs is
    removed.

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Downloads the 1 by 1 degree elevation tiles (about 39 MB each, at 30 m)
    covering your map regions from the Copernicus DEM on AWS open data,
    verifies each, and keeps them under
    /usr/local/share/hammunition/data/dem-copernicus/. Squares of open sea
    have no tile and are counted as sea. `dem-qmapshack` turns them into
    QMapShack's hillshade, slope and contours.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    Terrain is the difference between a line on a map and a walk you can
    judge: how steep, how high, which side of the ridge. Offline, it has to
    be on the disk first; this is the one place it is fetched, sized in the
    plan and verified.
  prerequisites: >-
    Map regions set in station config; the tiles follow them. With none set
    this unit is deferred by name.
  known_problems: >-
    A US state is tens of tiles, so hundreds of MB to a little over a GB;
    the plan prints the count and size per region before anything
    downloads. A tile with a row in catalog/data/copernicus-glo30-pins.yaml
    is checked against a sha256 Hammunition measured ("sha256, pinned by
    Hammunition"); any other only against the MD5 in the publisher's object
    metadata ("MD5 from the publisher's object metadata; not pinned by
    Hammunition"), which catches a damaged download, not a deliberately
    altered one. The first install of a region asks Geofabrik for its
    outline to choose its tiles and remembers the answer, so later plans
    need no network. Tile names encode latitude and longitude, so they are
    shown in the plan only. The licence requires attribution to the
    Copernicus programme in anything you publish from the data.
  upstream_url: https://registry.opendata.aws/copernicus-dem/
  upstream_support: >-
    The dataset's readme on the bucket
    (https://copernicus-dem-30m.s3.amazonaws.com/readme.html) and the
    Copernicus Space Data helpdesk.
```

`catalog/packages/dem-qmapshack.yaml`:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: dem-qmapshack
version: "station"
summary: Hillshade, slope and 20 m contour lines for QMapShack, from the elevation tiles
categories: [navigation-maps]

# Derived data (D-061): the `gdal-dem` converter builds QMapShack's DEM (a
# virtual raster over the installed tiles) and a contour overlay map.
depends: [dem-copernicus, gdal-bin]

install:
  - install:
      method: derived
      converter: gdal-dem
      source: dem-copernicus
      licence: Copernicus DEM licence (free, with attribution)
      licence_url: https://copernicus-dem-30m.s3.amazonaws.com/readme.html

update:
  probe:
    method: none
  strategy: reinstall
  cadence_hint: >-
    Follows dem-copernicus: a new tile gets contours on the next install and
    both virtual rasters are rebuilt when the set of tiles changes.

documentation:
  what_it_does: >-
    Builds two things QMapShack reads, under
    /usr/local/share/hammunition/data/dem-qmapshack/: `dem/dem.vrt`, one
    virtual raster over every elevation tile, from which QMapShack draws
    hillshade and slope and reads heights for track profiles; and
    `contours/contours.vrt`, plain 20 m contour lines drawn per tile with
    GDAL, which QMapShack lists as a map to lay over the others.
  why_you_want_it: >-
    QMapShack does not draw contour lines from elevation itself, and needs
    its elevation as one virtual raster. This makes both from the tiles,
    with the archive's GDAL and nothing else.
  prerequisites: >-
    `dem-copernicus` and `gdal-bin`, which install first.
  known_problems: >-
    The contours are unlabelled lines at a fixed resolution: legible, not
    pretty. Labelled contours inside the map need a tool the archive does
    not carry. Measured on one mountain tile on 2026-09-28: 4 s and a
    97.8 MB GeoPackage to trace, 1.5 s and 5.4 MB to rasterise; the
    GeoPackage is removed once rasterised. Hillshade drawing has not yet
    been confirmed on hardware.
  upstream_url: https://gdal.org/
  upstream_support: >-
    The GDAL documentation (https://gdal.org/) for the tools; QMapShack's
    wiki (https://github.com/Maproom/qmapshack/wiki) for how it shows them.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: The profile** — `catalog/profiles/navigation.yaml` becomes:

```yaml
# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright (C) 2026 Renegade Penguin LLC
# SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0

name: navigation
summary: Offline maps and turn-by-turn navigation from your own GPS, with no network
# post-1.0 until the maintainer places it: the 1.0 profile set is the one
# accepted on 2026-08-29 and asserted by tests/test_schema.py, and adding
# to it is a decision, not a side effect of a new profile.
stage: post-1.0

# The navigation profile, piece 1 (docs/superpowers/specs/
# 2026-09-27-navigation-maps-design.md): Navit over Geofabrik regions.
# Piece 2 (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-hiking-maps-design.md, D-061):
# QMapShack with trails, terrain and routing on foot. Piece 3 (Kiwix, tile
# servers) adds to it.
packages:
  - gpsd
  - gpsd-clients
  - navit
  - osm-regions
  - osm-navit
  - qmapshack
  - routino
  - gdal-bin
  - mkgmap
  - mkgmap-splitter
  - socat
  - osm-garmin
  - osm-routino
  - dem-copernicus
  - dem-qmapshack

documentation:
  what_it_installs: >-
    gpsd and its clients to read the GPS receiver; Navit, an offline
    turn-by-turn navigator with spoken directions through espeak-ng; the
    OpenStreetMap regions you choose, downloaded from Geofabrik and verified
    (`osm-regions`); and those regions converted into Navit's format with a
    Navit configuration that loads all of them and follows the GPS
    (`osm-navit`, opened by the `navit-offline` launcher). For trails and
    terrain: QMapShack, with Garmin maps built from the same regions
    (`osm-garmin`), one Routino database over all of them for routes on foot
    (`osm-routino`), Copernicus elevation tiles for the regions
    (`dem-copernicus`) and the hillshade, slope and contours drawn from them
    (`dem-qmapshack`), opened by the `qmapshack-offline` launcher; and
    `gps-tether`, which serves gpsd's position to QMapShack on this machine
    only.
  why_together: >-
    A navigator is only useful with a position and a map, and offline it
    needs both already on the machine. Navit without gpsd does not know
    where you are, and gpsd alone is a position with nothing to show it on.
    The same install serves daily driving and an EMCOMM deployment with the
    phone network down: the maps are on the laptop before anything goes
    wrong, and `hammunition update` says when newer map data is pinned.
  deliberately_excludes: >-
    Tile servers and Kiwix are the next piece of this work. Viking, Marble,
    GPSPrune and JOSM were measured and left out: Debian's Viking renders
    nothing offline, Marble's offline place index has no packaged builder,
    and GPSPrune and JOSM use online tiles. BRouter is out because its
    routing data is regenerated weekly with no checksum. Navit's GTK interface (`navit-gui-gtk`) is
    left out in favour of the internal one, which suits a touch screen and a
    small panel. Grid-square readouts belong to the station and dashboard
    tools.
  manual_configuration: >-
    Choose your map regions first, with Geofabrik's region paths, for
    example `hammunition station set --map-regions
    north-america/us/vermont,north-america/us/new-hampshire`; without them
    the map units are deferred and only Navit and gpsd install. Optionally
    `hammunition station set --map-freshness yearly|monthly|latest`
    (yearly is the default and, for the 50 US states and DC, is checked
    against a sha256 Hammunition measured; other regions and modes are
    checked against Geofabrik's MD5, and the plan says which for every
    region). A GPS receiver must be attached and seen by gpsd; `xgps`, from
    gpsd-clients, shows whether it has a fix. For QMapShack: start it from
    the `qmapshack-offline` launcher; in its Routino router choose the
    `hammunition` database and the `foot` profile; for your position, run
    `gps-tether` and point QMapShack's GPS Tether at 127.0.0.1 port 10110.
  disk_footprint_hint: >-
    Navit, espeak-ng and gpsd from the archive (size not yet measured).
    Per region: the download, plus Navit's converted map at about 0.9 times
    the download, plus about twice the download of temporary space while it
    converts (the map factor measured on three regions: a 6.1 GB
    country-sized region converted to 4.7 GB, about 0.77 times, in 75
    minutes on an i7-1185G7, and two US-state-sized regions at 0.874 and
    0.856 times; the scratch on the country-sized region only). A single US state's
    download ranges from about 20 MB (District of Columbia) to 1.3 GB
    (California). QMapShack's maps, measured on one US-state-sized region:
    the Garmin map at 0.85 times the download with up to three times it of
    scratch, and the Routino database over every region at 0.67 times all
    the downloads together with up to six times of scratch. Terrain: about
    39 MB per elevation tile, tens of tiles for a US state, and about 5.5 MB
    of contours per tile.
```

### Task 15: D-061, the guide, the CLI reference, SCOPE and the README

**Files:**
- Modify: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (append D-061), `docs/guides/offline-navigation.md`, `docs/reference/cli.md`, `docs/SCOPE.md` (stage 13), `README.md` (a row)
- Test: `scripts/check_doc_links.py`, the whole suite

- [ ] **Step 1: D-061** — append to `docs/DECISIONS.md`:

````markdown
## D-061 — Trails, terrain and routing on foot: QMapShack over Garmin maps and one Routino database built from the station's regions, and verified Copernicus elevation

**Date:** 2026-09-28. **Status:** accepted (maintainer, 2026-09-28, "sounds
good go for it", on `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-hiking-maps-design.md`),
with one departure from that specification put to the maintainer with the
implementation plan (tile selection, below). **Depends on:** D-057 (regions
are station data; derived data by a converter enum; MD5 disclosed, never
silent), D-049 (offline data is a catalog unit), D-035 and D-039 (a missing
station value defers by name), D-031 (verify the effect), D-043 (the
operator owns what is built on their behalf), D-021 (state the licence).
**Amends:** D-057's converter enum, with three members, and D-049's install
methods, with `dem-tiles`.

**Why.** The maintainer, 2026-09-28, after Navit ran on the field laptop:
"need maps for trails, and such too. Think survival and EMCOMM." Navit is a
road navigator; it has no terrain and draws trails as an afterthought. A
search, a hike to a repeater site or a deployment off the road network needs
trails, contours, a route on foot and the position on the same map.

### What is carried, and what was measured and left out

QMapShack (Parrot echo 1.17.1; 1.21.1 in backports, which D-038 takes where
the machine already installs from there), Routino 3.4.3, GDAL 3.10.3,
mkgmap r4923 with mkgmap-splitter r654, and socat, all from the archive;
nothing is built from source. Viking, Marble, GPSPrune and JOSM were measured
and are not carried: Debian's Viking links no Mapnik, so it renders nothing
offline, and routes only through web services; Marble's offline place index
has no packaged builder; GPSPrune and JOSM use online tiles.

| Unit | What | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `osm-garmin` | converter `mkgmap`: mkgmap-splitter, then mkgmap `--style=default --route --add-pois-to-areas --unicode --gmapsupp` | `data/osm-garmin/<slug>.img` |
| `osm-routino` | converter `routino-planetsplitter`: `--parse-only` per region (`--append` after the first), one `--process-only` | `data/osm-routino/hammunition-*.mem` |
| `dem-copernicus` | method `dem-tiles`, provider `copernicus-glo30` | `data/dem-copernicus/<tile>.tif` |
| `dem-qmapshack` | converter `gdal-dem`: `gdalbuildvrt` over the tiles; per tile `gdal_contour -i 20` → `gdal_rasterize` | `data/dem-qmapshack/{dem,contours}/` |

`--index` and `--housenumbers` are not passed to mkgmap: they need its
upstream bounds files, which have no published checksum, and QMapShack does
not read that index.

### One Routino database over every region

A route may cross from one region into the next only inside one database;
QMapShack's own help says Routino cannot route across two. The trade is
stated in the plan: a region that fails to parse fails the database, not
only itself, and the step names the region and why; the database already
installed is left as it was, and the record of what it was built from
(`hammunition.source`) is removed before new files are published, so a
publish interrupted halfway is rebuilt rather than trusted.

### Tiles follow each region's outline, and are verified in D-057's two modes

A tile is one 1°×1° square named by its south-west corner. Which squares
exist is the bucket's `tileList.txt`, carried as
`catalog/data/copernicus-glo30-tiles.txt` (26,450 names), so a square not in
it is sea and the plan needs no network to know it.

The specification chose each region's squares from the bounding box in its
`.osm.pbf` header. Measured before the build on the two regions installed on
the development host: for one of them that box spans hundreds of squares where
the region's Geofabrik outline touches tens, and does not contain the
outline's own box. The squares are therefore those the outline
(`<region>.poly`, what Geofabrik cut the extract with) touches, edge or
interior, asked once per region through the same probe that resolves the
regions, and remembered in `data/dem-copernicus/<slug>.tiles` so a later plan
needs no network. The header box also does not exist before a region's first
download, which is when the plan must print the tiles.

A tile with a row in `catalog/data/copernicus-glo30-pins.yaml` is checked
against the sha256 Hammunition measured, and the plan says **"sha256, pinned
by Hammunition"**; any other against the MD5 in the object's S3 ETag (a
single-part upload's MD5, measured equal to `md5sum` on a tile), and the plan
says **"MD5 from the publisher's object metadata; not pinned by
Hammunition"**, tile by tile. A multipart ETag is not an MD5, and such a tile
is refused by name. The pins grow as the maintainer runs
`scripts/gen_copernicus_pins.py --max-tiles N`: always the next tiles of one
fixed order, the 1,447 tiles the 50 US states and DC touch, state by state,
so the pin file never says whose region was pinned first. The weekly job
checks the carried list against the bucket's and every pin's size and ETag.

### Converters run as the operator, and fail into their own ledger

Every converter runs in its own staging directory under the operator's
`~/.cache/hammunition/build/`, through one `Staging` object: under `sudo`,
every staging-side operation is a process dropped to the operator, and the
staged output is published into the prefix only if it still hashes to what
the operator's process measured (piece 1's rule for maptool, made one class).
Outputs are checked, not exit statuses: mkgmap exits 0 while logging SEVERE,
and `gdal_contour` exits 0 appending to an existing file. A failure is
recorded in a terrain ledger separate from piece 1's: a region whose Garmin
map did not build is still a region Navit converts.

### QMapShack is told where the maps are, and nothing else changes

`hammunition maps qmapshack`, run by the `qmapshack-offline` launcher, adds
Hammunition's directories to QMapShack's own settings if they are absent —
`mapPath`, `demPaths` and `Route/routino/paths`, read from QMapShack 1.17.1's
binary — keeps every existing value, touches nothing else, refuses a file it
cannot read, and refuses root. Per-user and unprivileged, like the menu
files (D-050).

### The GPS tether is loopback only

QMapShack has no gpsd client; its GPS Tether reads NMEA over TCP.
`hammunition maps gps-tether`, the `gps-tether` launcher, runs `gpspipe -r`
behind `socat` listening on 127.0.0.1 port 10110 only, one client at a time,
while the operator runs it. A position is where the operator is; it is never
served to the network, and nothing is installed as a service.

### The plan, disk and privacy

The plan's *Map regions* section gains a *Terrain* block: tiles per region,
how many squares are sea and what the region's tiles cost to download, each tile to fetch with its size and
verification, and what is built for QMapShack with its estimate. The factors,
each printed with "measured on one region": Garmin map 0.85× the download
with 3× scratch; Routino database 0.67× all the downloads together with 6×
scratch (5.02× sampled at peak); contours about 5.5 MB a tile with up to
98 MB of scratch at a time. The disk check counts piece 1 and piece 2
together. Tile names encode latitude and longitude, so they are printed in
the plan only; `update` prints how many tiles are installed and nothing else.

### Gaps, documented and carried

- **Offline address search is Navit's.** QMapShack's search is online only;
  Routino takes coordinates. Find the address in Navit, walk it in QMapShack.
- **Trail difficulty is not routed on.** Routino's foot profile ignores
  `sac_scale`.
- **No hiking cartography.** No hiking style or TYP file is in the archive;
  OpenTopoMap's and Freizeitkarte's are unmeasured upstream projects.
- **Labelled contours inside the map** need a DEM-to-OSM tool the archive
  lacks (`pyhgtmap` on PyPI is the candidate); the raster overlay is legible,
  not pretty.
- **SRTM refused** (Earthdata login). **viewfinderpanoramas not carried**
  (no checksum, unclear licence). **USGS 3DEP** is the alternative provider
  if anyone asks; the `provider` enum has room for it.
- **BRouter** stays out: its jar is pinnable, its weekly routing data is not.

### What is measured, and what is not yet

Measured on the development host on 2026-09-28: every converter's argv and
factor above on one region or tile; the tile list; one live pin; the bridge
bound to 127.0.0.1. Not yet measured on the field laptop, and not claimed
until `docs/reference/bench-verification-5430.md` records it: QMapShack
drawing hillshade from the DEM, the settings keys taking effect, a route
across two regions, the tether with a real receiver, and the whole install.
````

- [ ] **Step 2: The guide** — in `docs/guides/offline-navigation.md`:

(a) In the opening paragraph, after "…with Navit following your GPS receiver through gpsd and speaking the turns." add: " For trails and terrain it also builds maps for QMapShack from the same regions, a routing database for walking, and elevation with contours (**D-061**)." and change "The decision record behind all of this is **D-057**" to "The decision records behind all of this are **D-057** and **D-061**".

(b) Insert before `## Disk planning`:

````markdown
## 8. Trails and terrain: QMapShack

The same install builds a second set of maps from the same regions, for
walking rather than driving:

- **Garmin maps** (`osm-garmin`) that show paths, footways, tracks and
  bridleways as well as roads, with land use and water;
- **one routing database** (`osm-routino`) over every region, so a route on
  foot can cross from one region into the next;
- **elevation** (`dem-copernicus`): the Copernicus 30 m elevation tiles for
  your regions, about 39 MB each, tens of them for a US state;
- **hillshade, slope and contour lines** (`dem-qmapshack`) drawn from that
  elevation.

The plan shows all of it before anything happens, in a *Terrain* block under
*Map regions*: how many tiles each region needs, how many of its squares are
open sea (no tile, nothing to download) and what its tiles cost to download,
each tile to fetch with its size
and how it is checked, and what will be built with its estimated size.
Tile names encode a latitude and a longitude, so they appear in the plan on
your own terminal and nowhere else.

Start QMapShack from its launcher, *QMapShack with your offline maps*, or:

```
qmapshack-offline
```

The launcher adds the map, elevation and routing directories to QMapShack's
own settings the first time, keeping anything you set there yourself, then
starts it. If it says it cannot read your settings file, nothing was changed;
add the directories by hand in QMapShack's setup, or move the file
(`~/.config/QLandkarte/QMapShack.conf`) aside and start it again.

In QMapShack:

- **Maps.** Your regions' Garmin maps and the contour map (from
  `contours.vrt`) are listed in the map dock. Activate a region's map, then
  the contour map over it.
- **Terrain.** The elevation is listed in the DEM dock; activate it and turn
  on hillshade or slope there.
- **Route on foot.** In the routing dock choose *Routino (offline)*, the
  database `hammunition`, and the profile `foot`. Place a start and an end on
  the map; the route follows trails. It came back in milliseconds for walks
  of 10 to 17 km when measured.
- **Tracks.** Record, load and edit GPX tracks and waypoints; any track shows
  an elevation profile from the elevation data.

Routino's foot profile does not read trail difficulty (`sac_scale`): an
alpine path counts the same as a pavement. Look at the contours and judge
the route yourself.

---

## 9. Find an address in Navit, walk it in QMapShack

QMapShack cannot search for an address offline: its search asks online
services, and Routino routes between points, not addresses. Navit can.

1. In `navit-offline`, open the menu, search for the town and street (and
   house number, where OpenStreetMap has one), and look at the result on the
   map. Note where it is — the coordinates Navit shows, or the nearest trail
   junction or landmark.
2. In QMapShack, place the route's end there and plan the walk.

For a named trailhead, Navit finds it only if it is tagged as a place in
OpenStreetMap; otherwise find the nearest road end in Navit and walk from
there in QMapShack.

---

## 10. Your position in QMapShack: the GPS tether

QMapShack does not talk to gpsd. Run the *GPS position for QMapShack*
launcher, or in a terminal:

```
hammunition maps gps-tether
```

It serves gpsd's position on this machine only — 127.0.0.1, port 10110 —
and prints what to enter. In QMapShack open *Realtime*, add *GPS Tether*,
host `127.0.0.1`, port `10110`. It runs while that terminal stays open;
Ctrl-C stops it. Nothing is installed as a service, and nothing else on the
network can connect to it. Navit reads gpsd directly and needs none of this.

---

## What QMapShack does not do (yet)

- **No offline address search** — use Navit (section 9).
- **Trail difficulty is not routed on** (section 8).
- **No hiking map style.** Trails are drawn by mkgmap's default style; no
  hiking style is packaged in the archive.
- **Contours are unlabelled lines**, legible rather than pretty. Labelled
  contours need a tool the archive does not carry.
- **The Garmin maps have no address index**, and QMapShack would not read
  one anyway.
- **Hillshade drawing** has not yet been confirmed on the field laptop.

---
````

(c) In `## Disk planning`, after the existing table add:

````markdown
With the QMapShack units, each region also takes, measured on one
US-state-sized region:

| What | How much | Where | How long it stays |
|---|---|---|---|
| The Garmin map | about 0.85× the region | `/usr/local/share/hammunition/data/osm-garmin/` | Until uninstall or the region is dropped |
| Its build scratch | up to 3× the region, one region at a time | `~/.cache/hammunition/build/osm-garmin/` | Only while it builds |
| The routing database, all regions | about 0.67× all the regions together | `/usr/local/share/hammunition/data/osm-routino/` | Rebuilt when the regions change |
| Its build scratch | up to 6× all the regions together | `~/.cache/hammunition/build/osm-routino/` | Only while it builds |
| Elevation tiles | about 39 MB a tile; tens for a US state | `/usr/local/share/hammunition/data/dem-copernicus/` | Until uninstall, or no region needs the tile |
| Contours | about 5.5 MB a tile, with up to 98 MB of scratch at a time | `/usr/local/share/hammunition/data/dem-qmapshack/` | As the tiles |

A tile's download is deleted from the cache as soon as it is installed: a
tile never changes, so there is no reason to keep two copies. The plan
counts all of it, and refuses before anything is fetched if a disk is short.
````

(d) In `## What the verification wording means`, after the two region phrases add:

````markdown
Elevation tiles use the same two levels, in their own words:

- **"sha256, pinned by Hammunition"** — the tile has a row in
  `catalog/data/copernicus-glo30-pins.yaml`, measured by Hammunition.
- **"MD5 from the publisher's object metadata; not pinned by Hammunition"**
  — checked against the MD5 the storage service keeps for the file. It
  catches a damaged download, not a deliberately altered one.
````

(e) In `## Removing it`, after the existing command add:

````markdown
The QMapShack maps, routing database and elevation go the same way, and
leave QMapShack installed:

```
hammunition uninstall dem-qmapshack dem-copernicus osm-routino osm-garmin
```

Your QMapShack settings keep the directories the launcher added; QMapShack
lists nothing there once they are gone.
````

(f) In `## What has not been measured yet`, replace the paragraph beginning "Hiking and topographic maps (QMapShack, contours)" with:

````markdown
For the QMapShack units (**D-061**), the converters and their sizes were
measured on one region on the development host; on the field laptop, not yet:

- QMapShack drawing hillshade and slope from the elevation.
- The settings the launcher writes taking effect in QMapShack 1.17.1 (or
  1.21.1 from backports).
- A route on foot across the boundary between two regions.
- The GPS tether with a real receiver.

Offline reference (Kiwix, a local tile server) is the next piece of this
work and not in this profile.
````

- [ ] **Step 3: The CLI reference** — in `docs/reference/cli.md`:

(a) Insert before `### \`hammunition list [all|packages|profiles]\``:

````markdown
### `hammunition maps qmapshack [--configure-only]`

What the `qmapshack-offline` launcher runs (**D-061**). Adds Hammunition's
map, elevation and routing directories to QMapShack's own settings,
`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/QLandkarte/QMapShack.conf` (by default
`~/.config/QLandkarte/QMapShack.conf`) — `mapPath`, `demPaths` and
`Route/routino/paths` — each only if absent, keeping every value already
there and changing nothing else, then starts `qmapshack`. `--configure-only`
edits and does not start it.

It refuses, exit 1, changing nothing and starting nothing, when the file
holds a line that is neither a `[section]` nor `key=value`, or one of those
keys holds a quoted or `@`-typed value; and under root, whose settings are
not the operator's. It prints a line to stderr when it changed the file.

### `hammunition maps gps-tether`

What the `gps-tether` launcher runs (**D-061**). Serves gpsd's raw NMEA
(`gpspipe -r`) through `socat` on **127.0.0.1 port 10110 only**, one client at
a time, for QMapShack's *Realtime → GPS Tether*, and prints the host and port
to enter. It runs in the foreground until Ctrl-C; nothing is installed as a
service. Needs `socat` and `gpsd-clients`; a missing `socat` is a named
error, exit 1.
````

(b) In the `install` section, before `**Recommends, per unit (D-052).**`, add:

````markdown
**Terrain and QMapShack's maps (D-061).** When the plan holds
`dem-copernicus`, `osm-garmin`, `osm-routino` or `dem-qmapshack`, the map
section gains a *Terrain* block. Before the plan prints, each region's tiles
are read from its record (`dem-copernicus/<slug>.tiles`) or, the first time,
chosen from its Geofabrik outline (`<region>.poly`); each tile not installed
is resolved from its pin, or by a `HEAD` to the bucket for its size and
ETag. From the golden test's synthetic plan:

```
  Terrain, Copernicus GLO-30 elevation (D-061):
    atlantis/oceania  2 tile(s), 2 square(s) of sea; 39.1 MB to download
    atlantis/lemuria  1 tile(s); 25.2 MB to download
    will be downloaded (2 tile(s), 64.3 MB):
      Copernicus_DSM_COG_10_N00_00_E000_00_DEM    39.1 MB  sha256, pinned by Hammunition
      Copernicus_DSM_COG_10_S01_00_W001_00_DEM    25.2 MB  MD5 from the publisher's object metadata; not pinned by Hammunition
    already installed: 1 tile(s)
      licence: Copernicus DEM licence, stated at https://spacedata.copernicus.eu/
  Built for QMapShack (sizes an estimate, measured on one region):
    Garmin map  atlantis/oceania  260101  about 44.6 MB (0.85x the download)
    Routino database over 2 region(s)  about 42.2 MB (0.67x the downloads together)
    contours for 2 tile(s)  about 11.0 MB, with up to 98.0 MB of scratch at a time
      about 0.16 GB of disk for terrain and QMapShack's maps (measured on one region)
```

The commands section shows each tile's fetch (all fetches first, as every
download is), each install, each region's record, each Garmin build and the
Routino build (as the operator, in `~/.cache/hammunition/build/osm-garmin/`
and `.../osm-routino/`), each tile's contours (`.../dem-qmapshack/`), the two
virtual rasters, and a last step that fails the run by name if anything of
this did not install. It refuses at plan time, exit 2, changing nothing, when
a region's outline or a tile not installed cannot be resolved (every such one
named together), when the carried tile list is missing, and when a disk is
short of piece 1's and piece 2's estimates together. With no regions set, all
four units are deferred by name with the rest of the map data.
````

````markdown
For `dem-copernicus` the row is a count, never a tile name — a tile name is
a latitude and longitude: `43 terrain tile(s) installed; a tile changes only
when the publisher's tile list does`, or `not installed`. When `osm-regions`
is behind, the footer's command also names `osm-garmin` and `osm-routino` if
````

````markdown
The **qmapshack** check (**D-061**) appears only when `qmapshack` is on the
`PATH` and `/usr/share/routino/translations.xml` is missing: QMapShack stops
at startup with "The specified translations XML file did not exist" until it
is back. It is a warn, with `sudo apt-get install --reinstall routino-common`
as the fix.
````
